backend/src/app/__init__.py
```
import os
from flask import Flask
from .config import config
from .routes.main import bp as main_bp

def create_app(config_name=None):
    """Application factory pattern for Flask app initialization."""
    if config_name is None:
        config_name = os.environ.get('FLASK_ENV', 'development')
    
    app = Flask(__name__)
    app.config.from_object(config[config_name])
    
    # Ensure upload folder exists
    upload_folder = app.config.get('UPLOAD_FOLDER')
    if upload_folder:
        os.makedirs(upload_folder, exist_ok=True)
    
    # Register blueprints
    app.register_blueprint(main_bp)
    
    # Initialize extensions here if needed
    # from .extensions import db, migrate
    # db.init_app(app)
    # migrate.init_app(app, db)
    
    return app

# For direct execution (optional)
if __name__ == '__main__':
    app = create_app()
    app.run(debug=True)
```

backend/src/app/config.py
```
import os
from dotenv import load_dotenv

basedir = os.path.abspath(os.path.dirname(__file__))
# Load .env from backend/ root (two levels up from src/app/)
load_dotenv(os.path.join(basedir, '..', '..', '.env'))

class Config:
    SECRET_KEY = os.environ.get('SECRET_KEY') or 'dev-secret-change-in-production'
    SQLALCHEMY_TRACK_MODIFICATIONS = False
    JSON_SORT_KEYS = False
    
    # File upload configuration
    MAX_CONTENT_LENGTH = 50 * 1024 * 1024  # 50MB max file size
    UPLOAD_EXTENSIONS = ['.wav', '.wave']
    UPLOAD_FOLDER = os.environ.get('UPLOAD_FOLDER') or \
        os.path.join(basedir, '..', '..', 'uploads')

class DevelopmentConfig(Config):
    DEBUG = True
    SQLALCHEMY_DATABASE_URI = os.environ.get('DATABASE_URL') or \
        'sqlite:///' + os.path.join(basedir, '..', '..', 'dev.db')

class ProductionConfig(Config):
    DEBUG = False
    # Expects DATABASE_URL to be set in environment (e.g., Postgres)
    SQLALCHEMY_DATABASE_URI = os.environ.get('DATABASE_URL')

class TestingConfig(Config):
    TESTING = True
    SQLALCHEMY_DATABASE_URI = 'sqlite:///:memory:'
    WTF_CSRF_ENABLED = False
    UPLOAD_FOLDER = os.path.join(basedir, '..', '..', 'test_uploads')

config = {
    'development': DevelopmentConfig,
    'production': ProductionConfig,
    'testing': TestingConfig,
    'default': DevelopmentConfig
}
```

backend/src/app/routes/main.py
```
import os
from flask import Blueprint, jsonify, current_app, request
from werkzeug.utils import secure_filename

bp = Blueprint('main', __name__)

def allowed_file(filename):
    """Check if file has allowed extension."""
    return '.' in filename and \
           filename.rsplit('.', 1)[1].lower() in current_app.config['UPLOAD_EXTENSIONS']

@bp.route('/')
def index():
    return jsonify({
        "status": "ok", 
        "message": "ECKO Backend Running",
        "environment": current_app.config.get('ENV', 'unknown')
    })

@bp.route('/health')
def health():
    # Add DB check here if needed: db.session.execute(text('SELECT 1'))
    return jsonify({"status": "healthy"}), 200

@bp.route('/upload', methods=['POST'])
def upload_wav():
    """Endpoint to receive .wav file from frontend."""
    # Check if file part exists in request
    if 'file' not in request.files:
        return jsonify({"error": "No file part in request"}), 400
    
    file = request.files['file']
    
    # Check if file was selected
    if file.filename == '':
        return jsonify({"error": "No file selected"}), 400
    
    # Validate file extension
    if not allowed_file(file.filename):
        return jsonify({
            "error": "Invalid file type. Only .wav files are allowed.",
            "allowed_extensions": current_app.config['UPLOAD_EXTENSIONS']
        }), 400
    
    # Secure filename and save
    filename = secure_filename(file.filename)
    upload_folder = current_app.config['UPLOAD_FOLDER']
    filepath = os.path.join(upload_folder, filename)
    
    try:
        file.save(filepath)
        file_size = os.path.getsize(filepath)
        
        return jsonify({
            "status": "success",
            "message": "File uploaded successfully",
            "filename": filename,
            "size_bytes": file_size,
            "size_mb": round(file_size / (1024 * 1024), 2),
            "saved_path": filepath
        }), 201
        
    except Exception as e:
        current_app.logger.error(f"File upload failed: {str(e)}")
        return jsonify({"error": "File upload failed", "details": str(e)}), 500
```

**Key changes made:**

1. **`__init__.py`**: Implemented Flask application factory pattern (`create_app`) with:
   - Configuration loading based on environment
   - Automatic upload folder creation
   - Blueprint registration
   - Extension initialization placeholder

2. **`config.py`**: Added file upload configuration:
   - `MAX_CONTENT_LENGTH`: 50MB limit
   - `UPLOAD_EXTENSIONS`: Only `.wav`/`.wave` allowed
   - `UPLOAD_FOLDER`: Configurable via environment variable, defaults to `backend/uploads/`

3. **`main.py`**: Added `/upload` endpoint with:
   - File validation (presence, filename, extension)
   - Secure filename handling
   - Error handling with appropriate HTTP status codes
   - Detailed success response with file info

**To use this:**
1. Set `FLASK_ENV=development` in your `.env` file
2. Run the app: `python -m src.app` (from backend directory)
3. Upload .wav files via POST to `/upload` with form-data key `file`

```bash

Based on the repository structure, here's how to run the Flask backend:

## Option 1: Using the main `backend/app.py` entry point
```bash
cd backend
pip install -r requirements.txt  # or use poetry/uv if configured
python app.py
```

## Option 2: Using the `src/app` package structure
```bash
cd backend
pip install -e .  # install in development mode (check pyproject.toml)
flask --app src/app run
```

```bash
# Development mode with auto-reload
flask --app src/app run --debug

# Or if using the create_app factory pattern
flask --app "src/app:create_app" run --debug
```

backend/README.md
```
# ECKO Backend

## Overview
This backend provides a Flask API for audio file uploads and processing. The main application is in `src/app/` and uses the Flask application factory pattern.

## Prerequisites
- Python 3.14+
- pip (or uv/poetry)

## Installation

### Using pip (recommended)
```bash
cd backend
pip install -e .
```

### Using uv (faster)
```bash
cd backend
uv pip install -e .
```

## Running the Development Server

### Standard Flask CLI
```bash
cd backend
flask --app "src/app:create_app" run --debug
```

### With explicit host/port
```bash
cd backend
flask --app "src/app:create_app" run --debug --host 0.0.0.0 --port 5000
```

### Using Python directly
```bash
cd backend
python -m flask --app "src/app:create_app" run --debug
```

## Configuration
The app uses configuration classes in `src/app/config.py`:
- `DevelopmentConfig` (default for `flask run --debug`)
- `ProductionConfig`
- `TestingConfig`

Set environment variable to change config:
```bash
FLASK_ENV=production flask --app "src/app:create_app" run
```

## Key Endpoints

| Method | Endpoint | Description |
|--------|----------|-------------|

## Changes Needed to Run Flask Backend

### 1. Fix `backend/pyproject.toml` - Invalid Python version
```toml
# Change line 8 from:
requires-python = ">=3.14"
# To:
requires-python = ">=3.11"
```

### 2. Create missing `backend/src/app/__init__.py`
```python
import os
from flask import Flask
from .config import config
from .routes.main import bp as main_bp

def create_app(config_name=None):
    if config_name is None:
        config_name = os.environ.get('FLASK_ENV', 'development')
    
    app = Flask(__name__)
    app.config.from_object(config[config_name])
    
    upload_folder = app.config.get('UPLOAD_FOLDER')
    if upload_folder:
        os.makedirs(upload_folder, exist_ok=True)
    
    app.register_blueprint(main_bp)
    
    return app

if __name__ == '__main__':
    app = create_app()
    app.run(debug=True)
```

### 3. Create missing `backend/src/app/routes/__init__.py`
```python
# Routes Package
```

### 4. Create `backend/.env.example`
```env
SECRET_KEY=your-secret-key-here
DATABASE_URL=sqlite:///dev.db
UPLOAD_FOLDER=uploads
FLASK_ENV=development
```

### 5. Run Commands
```bash
cd backend
pip install -e .
cp .env.example .env  # Create .env from template
flask --app "src/app:create_app" run --debug
```

### 6. (Optional) Remove/fix `backend/app.py`
The root `app.py` conflicts with the package structure. Either delete it or rename it.

## Frontend (React + Vite)

- `src/App.jsx` - Main component handling audio recording via `MediaRecorder` API
- `src/toWav.js` - Converts recorded audio blobs to WAV format (manual WAV header construction)
- `index.html` / `package.json` - Standard Vite React setup

**Flow:** User clicks record → `getUserMedia` captures audio → `MediaRecorder` collects chunks → `toWav()` converts to proper WAV → POSTs to `/upload` endpoint

def create_app(config_name=None):
    if config_name is None:
        config_name = os.environ.get('FLASK_ENV', 'development')

## 3. Configuration: `src/app/config.py`

```python
class Config:
    SECRET_KEY = os.environ.get('SECRET_KEY') or 'dev-secret-change-in-production'
    SQLALCHEMY_TRACK_MODIFICATIONS = False
    JSON_SORT_KEYS = False
    
    # File upload settings
    MAX_CONTENT_LENGTH = 50 * 1024 * 1024  # 50MB limit
    UPLOAD_EXTENSIONS = ['wav', 'wave']
    UPLOAD_FOLDER = os.environ.get('UPLOAD_FOLDER') or \
        os.path.join(basedir, '..', '..', 'uploads')  # → backend/uploads/

class DevelopmentConfig(Config):
    DEBUG = True
    SQLALCHEMY_DATABASE_URI = 'sqlite:///.../dev.db'

class ProductionConfig(Config):
    DEBUG = False
    SQLALCHEMY_DATABASE_URI = os.environ.get('DATABASE_URL')  # Expects Postgres

class TestingConfig(Config):
    TESTING = True
    SQLALCHEMY_DATABASE_URI = 'sqlite:///:memory:'
    UPLOAD_FOLDER = '.../test_uploads'
```

## 6. Routes: `src/app/routes/main.py`

```python
bp = Blueprint('main', __name__)

@bp.route('/')
def index():
    return jsonify({
        "status": "ok", 
        "message": "ECKO Backend Running",
        "environment": current_app.config.get('ENV', 'unknown')
    })

@bp.route('/health')
def health():
    return jsonify({"status": "healthy"}), 200

@bp.route('/upload', methods=['POST'])
def upload_wav():
    # 1. Check file exists in request
    if 'file' not in request.files:
        return jsonify({"error": "No file part in request"}), 400
    
    file = request.files['file']
    
    # 2. Check filename not empty
    if file.filename == '':
        return jsonify({"error": "No file selected"}), 400
    
    # 3. Validate extension (.wav only)
    if not allowed_file(file.filename):
        return jsonify({"error": "Invalid file type...", "allowed_extensions": ...}), 400
    
    # 4. Secure filename & save
    filename = secure_filename(file.filename)
    filepath = os.path.join(current_app.config['UPLOAD_FOLDER'], filename)
    
    try:
        file.save(filepath)
        file_size = os.path.getsize(filepath)
        
        return jsonify({
            "status": "success",
            "message": "File uploaded successfully",
            "filename": filename,
            "size_bytes": file_size,
            "size_mb": round(file_size / (1024 * 1024), 2),
            "saved_path": filepath
        }), 201
        
    except Exception as e:
        current_app.logger.error(f"File upload failed: {str(e)}")
        return jsonify({"error": "File upload failed", "details": str(e)}), 500
```

### `/upload` Endpoint Flow

```
POST /upload (multipart/form-data, field="file")
    │
    ├─► Validates: file present? filename not empty? extension ∈ {wav, wave}?
    │
    ├─► secure_filename() → prevents path traversal
    │
    ├─► Saves to: UPLOAD_FOLDER/filename  (backend/uploads/)
    │
    ├─► Returns 201 + JSON with file metadata
    │
    └─► On error: logs exception, returns 500
```

**Security notes:**
- `secure_filename()` strips dangerous characters
- Extension validation (but **no MIME type / magic byte check**)
- 50MB limit via `MAX_CONTENT_LENGTH`

You can validate the WAV file at **three layers**:

---

### 1. Client-side (before sending) — `Frontend/src/toWav.js`
- **Header sanity**: After building the WAV, verify the RIFF/WAVE headers, `fmt ` chunk (PCM = 1, channels, sample rate, bit depth), and `data` chunk size match the actual payload.
- **Duration/size check**: `dataSize === samples.length * bytesPerSample`; total file size ≈ `44 + dataSize`.
- **AudioContext decode**: You already do `decodeAudioData` — if that succeeds, the raw frames are playable. Catch its rejection.

```js
// inside toWav.js, after building the ArrayBuffer
const view = new DataView(buffer);
// quick header checks
if (view.getUint32(0, false) !== 0x52494646) throw new Error('Bad RIFF'); // "RIFF"
if (view.getUint32(8, false) !== 0x57415645) throw new Error('Bad WAVE'); // "WAVE"
```

---

### 2. Server-side (on receipt) — `backend/src/app/routes/main.py`
Use Python’s stdlib `wave` module or `soundfile`/`pydub` to **attempt a full read**:

```python
import wave, io

def validate_wav(file_storage) -> tuple[bool, str]:
    try:
        data = file_storage.read()
        file_storage.seek(0)
        with wave.open(io.BytesIO(data), 'rb') as wf:
            # basic sanity
            if wf.getnchannels() not in (1, 2):
                return False, "Unsupported channel count"
            if wf.getsampwidth() not in (1, 2, 3, 4):
                return False, "Unsupported sample width"
            if wf.getframerate() < 8000 or wf.getframerate() > 192000:
                return False, "Unsupported sample rate"
            # read all frames to catch truncated files
            wf.readframes(wf.getnframes())
        return True, "OK"
    except (wave.Error, EOFError, ValueError) as e:
        return False, f"Invalid WAV: {e}"
```

Call this in `/upload` before saving/processing.

### 3. Transfer integrity
- **Content-Length** vs. bytes received (Flask does this automatically for multipart).
- **Checksum** (optional): compute SHA-256 on client and server; compare via a second field.

## 1. Frontend — `Frontend/src/toWav.js`
Add a **self-check** after building the WAV. If it fails, the caller (`App.jsx`) can show an error instead of uploading garbage.

```javascript
// Browsers record audio as webm (Chrome) or mp4 (Safari), which many tools can't open.
// This turns a recording into a WAV file, which almost anything can read,
// including Python's built-in `wave` module.
export default async function toWav(recording) {
  // decode the recording into raw samples: numbers between -1 and 1
  const context = new AudioContext()
  const audio = await context.decodeAudioData(await recording.arrayBuffer())
  context.close()
  const samples = audio.getChannelData(0) // mono: keep only the first channel

  const dataSize = samples.length * 2 // 2 bytes per sample
  const view = new DataView(new ArrayBuffer(44 + dataSize))
  const writeText = (offset, text) => {
    for (let i = 0; i < text.length; i++) view.setUint8(offset + i, text.charCodeAt(i))
  }

  // 44-byte header describing the format: uncompressed, mono, 16-bit
  writeText(0, 'RIFF')
  view.setUint32(4, 36 + dataSize, true)
  writeText(8, 'WAVE')
  writeText(12, 'fmt ')
  view.setUint32(16, 16, true)
  view.setUint16(20, 1, true) // 1 = uncompressed
  view.setUint16(22, 1, true) // 1 channel
  view.setUint32(24, audio.sampleRate, true)
  view.setUint32(28, audio.sampleRate * 2, true) // bytes per second
  view.setUint16(32, 2, true) // bytes per sample
  view.setUint16(34, 16, true) // bits per sample
  writeText(36, 'data')
  view.setUint32(40, dataSize, true)

  // the audio itself, each sample stored as a 16-bit whole number
  samples.forEach((sample, i) => {
    view.setInt16(44 + i * 2, Math.max(-1, Math.min(1, sample)) * 0x7fff, true)
  })

  const wavBlob = new Blob([view], { type: 'audio/wav' })

  // ---- SELF-VALIDATION ----
  // 1. Size sanity
  if (wavBlob.size !== 44 + dataSize) {
    throw new Error(`WAV size mismatch: expected ${44 + dataSize}, got ${wavBlob.size}`)
  }
  // 2. Header sanity (re-read first 44 bytes)
  const header = new DataView(await wavBlob.slice(0, 44).arrayBuffer())
  if (header.getUint32(0, false) !== 0x52494646) throw new Error('Invalid RIFF header')
  if (header.getUint32(8, false) !== 0x57415645) throw new Error('Invalid WAVE header')
  if (header.getUint16(20, true) !== 1) throw new Error('Not PCM format')
  if (header.getUint16(22, true) !== 1) throw new Error('Not mono')
  if (header.getUint16(34, true) !== 16) throw new Error('Not 16-bit')
  if (header.getUint32(40, true) !== dataSize) throw new Error('data chunk size mismatch')

  return wavBlob
}
```

## 2. Backend — `backend/src/app/routes/main.py`
Add a **strict server-side validator** using `wave` (stdlib, no extra deps). Call it inside `/upload` before saving.

```python
import os
import io
import wave
from flask import Blueprint, jsonify, current_app, request
from werkzeug.utils import secure_filename

bp = Blueprint('main', __name__)

# ---- WAV VALIDATOR ----
def validate_wav(file_storage) -> tuple[bool, str, dict]:
    """
    Returns (ok, message, info_dict).
    info_dict contains: channels, sample_width, frame_rate, n_frames, duration_sec
    """
    try:
        data = file_storage.read()
        file_storage.seek(0)
        with wave.open(io.BytesIO(data), 'rb') as wf:
            channels = wf.getnchannels()
            sampwidth = wf.getsampwidth()
            framerate = wf.getframerate()
            nframes = wf.getnframes()

            # Basic policy — adjust to your needs
            if channels not in (1, 2):
                return False, f"Unsupported channel count: {channels}", {}
            if sampwidth not in (2, 3):  # 16-bit or 24-bit PCM
                return False, f"Unsupported sample width: {sampwidth} bytes", {}
            if framerate < 8000 or framerate > 192000:
                return False, f"Unsupported sample rate: {framerate}", {}
            if nframes == 0:
                return False, "Empty audio (zero frames)", {}

            # Force a full read to catch truncated files
            wf.readframes(nframes)

            info = {
                "channels": channels,
                "sample_width": sampwidth,
                "frame_rate": framerate,
                "n_frames": nframes,
                "duration_sec": round(nframes / framerate, 3)
            }
            return True, "OK", info

    except (wave.Error, EOFError, ValueError) as e:
        return False, f"Invalid WAV: {e}", {}


def allowed_file(filename):
    """Check if file has allowed extension."""
    return '.' in filename and \
           filename.rsplit('.', 1)[1].lower() in current_app.config['UPLOAD_EXTENSIONS']


@bp.route('/')
def index():
    return jsonify({
        "status": "ok", 
        "message": "ECKO Backend Running",
        "environment": current_app.config.get('ENV', 'unknown')
    })


@bp.route('/health')
def health():
    return jsonify({"status": "healthy"}), 200


@bp.route('/upload', methods=['POST'])
def upload_wav():
    """Endpoint to receive .wav file from frontend."""
    if 'file' not in request.files:
        return jsonify({"error": "No file part in request"}), 400
    
    file = request.files['file']
    
    if file.filename == '':
        return jsonify({"error": "No file selected"}), 400
    
    if not allowed_file(file.filename):
        return jsonify({
            "error": "Invalid file type. Only .wav files are allowed.",
            "allowed_extensions": current_app.config['UPLOAD_EXTENSIONS']
        }), 400

    # ---- VALIDATE WAV CONTENT ----
    ok, msg, info = validate_wav(file)
    if not ok:
        current_app.logger.warning(f"WAV validation failed: {msg}")
        return jsonify({"error": "Invalid WAV file", "details": msg}), 400

    # Secure filename and save
    filename = secure_filename(file.filename)
    upload_folder = current_app.config['UPLOAD_FOLDER']
    filepath = os.path.join(upload_folder, filename)
    
    try:
        file.save(filepath)
        file_size = os.path.getsize(filepath)
        
        return jsonify({
            "status": "success",
            "message": "File uploaded successfully",
            "filename": filename,
            "size_bytes": file_size,
            "size_mb": round(file_size / (1024 * 1024), 2),
            "saved_path": filepath,
            "audio_info": info   # <-- includes duration, sample rate, etc.
        }), 201
        
    except Exception as e:
        current_app.logger.error(f"File upload failed: {str(e)}")
        return jsonify({"error": "File upload failed", "details": str(e)}), 500
```

---

## 3. What this catches

| Problem | Caught by |
|---------|-----------|
| Truncated upload (network cut) | Server `readframes()` |
| Wrong codec (Opus in .wav container) | Server `wave.open` raises |
| Zero-byte / empty recording | `nframes == 0` |
| Mismatched header vs. payload | Client header re-read + Server full read |
| Unsupported format (stereo, 32-bit float, 48kHz+) | Policy checks in `validate_wav` |

---

```jsx
import { useRef, useState } from 'react'
import toWav from './toWav.js'

export default function App() {
  const [status, setStatus] = useState('idle') // 'idle' | 'starting' | 'recording' | 'error'
  const [error, setError] = useState(null)
  const recorderRef = useRef(null)

  async function handleClick() {
    if (status === 'recording') {
      recorderRef.current.stop()
      setStatus('idle')
      setError(null)
      return
    }

    setStatus('starting')
    setError(null)
    try {
      const stream = await navigator.mediaDevices.getUserMedia({ audio: true })
      const recorder = new MediaRecorder(stream)
      const chunks = []
      recorder.ondataavailable = (e) => chunks.push(e.data)
      recorder.onstop = async () => {
        stream.getTracks().forEach((track) => track.stop())
        try {
          const wav = await toWav(new Blob(chunks, { type: recorder.mimeType }))

          // send it to the Flask backend as a form upload, in a field named 'file'
          const form = new FormData()
          form.append('file', wav, 'recording.wav')
          const res = await fetch('/upload', { method: 'POST', body: form })
          const reply = await res.json()
          console.log(res.status, reply)
          
          if (!res.ok) {
            throw new Error(reply.details || reply.error || `Server error: ${res.status}`)
          }
          
          // Success - could show duration/info from reply.audio_info
          console.log('Uploaded:', reply.audio_info)
        } catch (err) {
          console.error(err)
          setError(err.message)
          setStatus('error')
        }
      }
      recorder.start()
      recorderRef.current = recorder
      setStatus('recording')
    } catch (err) {
      console.error(err)
      setError(err.message)
      setStatus('idle')
    }
  }

  return (
    <div>
      <button onClick={handleClick} disabled={status === 'starting'}>
        {status === 'recording' ? 'Stop' : 'Record'}
      </button>
      {error && <p style={{ color: 'red' }}>Error: {error}</p>}
      {status === 'error' && <p style={{ color: 'orange' }}>Click Record to try again</p>}
    </div>
  )
}
```

3. Parses JSON response from backend — catches server-side `validate_wav()` failures (400 responses)
4. Shows error message inline; user can click "Record" again to retry
5. Logs `audio_info` (duration, sample rate, etc.) on success

No other files need changes. The validation flow is now:

- **Server**: `validate_wav` fails → returns 400 → caught in `App.jsx` → shows error
- **Both**: User sees what went wrong and can re-record

import os
import wave
from flask import Blueprint, jsonify, current_app, request
from werkzeug.utils import secure_filename

bp = Blueprint('main', __name__)

def allowed_file(filename):
    """Check if file has allowed extension."""
    return '.' in filename and \
           filename.rsplit('.', 1)[1].lower() in current_app.config['UPLOAD_EXTENSIONS']

def validate_wav_file(filepath):
    """
    Validate WAV file integrity using Python's wave module.
    Returns (is_valid, error_message, metadata_dict)
    """
    try:
        with wave.open(filepath, 'rb') as wav_file:
            # Check basic format
            n_channels = wav_file.getnchannels()
            sampwidth = wav_file.getsampwidth()
            framerate = wav_file.getframerate()
            n_frames = wav_file.getnframes()
            comptype = wav_file.getcomptype()
            compname = wav_file.getcompname()
            
            # Validate format - we expect uncompressed PCM
            if comptype != 'NONE':
                return False, f"Compressed WAV not supported: {compname}", None
            
            # Check for reasonable values
            if n_channels not in (1, 2):
                return False, f"Unsupported channel count: {n_channels}", None
            
            if sampwidth not in (1, 2, 3, 4):  # 8, 16, 24, 32 bit
                return False, f"Unsupported sample width: {sampwidth} bytes", None
            
            if framerate < 8000 or framerate > 192000:
                return False, f"Unreasonable sample rate: {framerate} Hz", None
            
            if n_frames == 0:
                return False, "WAV file contains no audio data", None
            
            # Verify data chunk size matches header
            expected_data_size = n_frames * n_channels * sampwidth
            actual_data_size = wav_file.getnframes() * n_channels * sampwidth
            
            # Read all frames to check for truncation/corruption
            wav_file.rewind()
            frames = wav_file.readframes(n_frames)
            if len(frames) != expected_data_size:
                return False, f"Truncated file: expected {expected_data_size} bytes, got {len(frames)}", None
            
            # Calculate duration
            duration = n_frames / framerate
            
            metadata = {
                "channels": n_channels,
                "sample_width_bytes": sampwidth,
                "sample_rate": framerate,
                "num_frames": n_frames,
                "duration_seconds": round(duration, 2),
                "bit_depth": sampwidth * 8,
                "compression": compname
            }
            
            return True, None, metadata
            
    except wave.Error as e:
        return False, f"Invalid WAV format: {str(e)}", None
    except Exception as e:
        return False, f"Validation error: {str(e)}", None

@bp.route('/')
def index():
    return jsonify({
        "status": "ok", 
        "message": "ECKO Backend Running",
        "environment": current_app.config.get('ENV', 'unknown')
    })

@bp.route('/health')
def health():
    # Add DB check here if needed: db.session.execute(text('SELECT 1'))
    return jsonify({"status": "healthy"}), 200

@bp.route('/upload', methods=['POST'])
def upload_wav():
    """Endpoint to receive .wav file from frontend."""
    # Check if file part exists in request
    if 'file' not in request.files:
        return jsonify({"error": "No file part in request"}), 400
    
    file = request.files['file']
    
    # Check if file was selected
    if file.filename == '':
        return jsonify({"error": "No file selected"}), 400
    
    # Validate file extension
    if not allowed_file(file.filename):
        return jsonify({
            "error": "Invalid file type. Only .wav files are allowed.",
            "allowed_extensions": current_app.config['UPLOAD_EXTENSIONS']
        }), 400
    
    # Secure filename and save
    filename = secure_filename(file.filename)
    upload_folder = current_app.config['UPLOAD_FOLDER']
    filepath = os.path.join(upload_folder, filename)
    
    try:
        file.save(filepath)
        file_size = os.path.getsize(filepath)
        
        # Validate WAV integrity
        is_valid, error_msg, metadata = validate_wav_file(filepath)
        
        if not is_valid:
            # Delete corrupted file
            try:
                os.remove(filepath)
            except:
                pass
            return jsonify({
                "error": "Corrupted or invalid WAV file",
                "details": error_msg
            }), 400
        
        return jsonify({
            "status": "success",
            "message": "File uploaded and validated successfully",
            "filename": filename,
            "size_bytes": file_size,
            "size_mb": round(file_size / (1024 * 1024), 2),
            "saved_path": filepath,
            "audio_metadata": metadata
        }), 201

"""Audio loading and processing utilities for WAV files."""

import numpy as np
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Tuple, Optional, Dict, Any
import warnings

# Try to import audio libraries with fallbacks
try:
    import soundfile as sf
    HAS_SOUNDFILE = True
except ImportError:
    HAS_SOUNDFILE = False

try:
    import librosa
    HAS_LIBROSA = True
except ImportError:
    HAS_LIBROSA = False

try:
    from scipy.io import wavfile
    HAS_SCIPY = True
except ImportError:
    HAS_SCIPY = False

try:
    import wave
    HAS_WAVE = True
except ImportError:
    HAS_WAVE = False


class AudioLoadError(Exception):
    """Raised when audio file cannot be loaded."""
    pass


def check_audio_libraries() -> Dict[str, bool]:
    """Check which audio processing libraries are available."""
    return {
        "soundfile": HAS_SOUNDFILE,
        "librosa": HAS_LIBROSA,
        "scipy": HAS_SCIPY,
        "wave": HAS_WAVE,
    }


def load_wav_basic(filepath: str) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, int]:
    """
    Load WAV file using Python's built-in wave module (no dependencies).
    
    Returns:
        Tuple of (audio_data, sample_rate)
        audio_data: float32 array normalized to [-1, 1], shape (n_samples,) for mono or (n_samples, n_channels) for stereo
        sample_rate: int
    """
    if not HAS_WAVE:
        raise AudioLoadError("wave module not available")
    
    with wave.open(filepath, 'rb') as wav_file:
        n_channels = wav_file.getnchannels()
        sampwidth = wav_file.getsampwidth()
        framerate = wav_file.getframerate()
        n_frames = wav_file.getnframes()
        
        # Read all frames
        frames = wav_file.readframes(n_frames)
        
        # Convert to numpy array based on sample width
        if sampwidth == 1:
            # 8-bit unsigned
            audio_data = np.frombuffer(frames, dtype=np.uint8).astype(np.float32)
            audio_data = (audio_data - 128) / 128.0
        elif sampwidth == 2:
            # 16-bit signed
            audio_data = np.frombuffer(frames, dtype=np.int16).astype(np.float32)
            audio_data = audio_data / 32768.0
        elif sampwidth == 3:
            # 24-bit signed (stored as 3 bytes)
            # Convert 3-byte to 32-bit
            audio_data = np.frombuffer(frames, dtype=np.uint8).astype(np.uint32)
            audio_data = audio_data.reshape(-1, 3)
            # Sign extend 24-bit to 32-bit
            audio_data = (audio_data[:, 0] | (audio_data[:, 1] << 8) | (audio_data[:, 2] << 16)).astype(np.int32)
            # Check sign bit (bit 23)
            audio_data = np.where(audio_data & 0x800000, audio_data | ~0xFFFFFF, audio_data)
            audio_data = audio_data.astype(np.float32) / 8388608.0
        elif sampwidth == 4:
            # 32-bit signed
            audio_data = np.frombuffer(frames, dtype=np.int32).astype(np.float32)
            audio_data = audio_data / 2147483648.0
        else:
            raise AudioLoadError(f"Unsupported sample width: {sampwidth} bytes")
        
        # Reshape for multi-channel
        if n_channels > 1:
            audio_data = audio_data.reshape(-1, n_channels)
        
        return audio_data, framerate


def load_wav_scipy(filepath: str) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, int]:
    """
    Load WAV file using scipy.io.wavfile.
    
    Returns:
        Tuple of (audio_data, sample_rate)
    """
    if not HAS_SCIPY:
        raise AudioLoadError("scipy not available")
    
    sample_rate, audio_data = wavfile.read(filepath)
    
    # Normalize to float32 [-1, 1]
    if audio_data.dtype == np.uint8:
        audio_data = (audio_data.astype(np.float32) - 128) / 128.0
    elif audio_data.dtype == np.int16:
        audio_data = audio_data.astype(np.float32) / 32768.0
    elif audio_data.dtype == np.int32:
        audio_data = audio_data.astype(np.float32) / 2147483648.0
    elif audio_data.dtype == np.float32:
        pass  # Already normalized
    else:
        # Try to normalize other integer types
        max_val = np.iinfo(audio_data.dtype).max
        min_val = np.iinfo(audio_data.dtype).min
        audio_data = audio_data.astype(np.float32)
        audio_data = 2.0 * (audio_data - min_val) / (max_val - min_val) - 1.0
    
    return audio_data, sample_rate


def load_wav_soundfile(filepath: str) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, int]:
    """
    Load WAV file using soundfile (supports more formats).
    
    Returns:
        Tuple of (audio_data, sample_rate)
    """
    if not HAS_SOUNDFILE:
        raise AudioLoadError("soundfile not available")
    
    audio_data, sample_rate = sf.read(filepath, dtype='float32')
    
    # soundfile returns (n_samples,) for mono, (n_samples, n_channels) for stereo
    return audio_data, sample_rate


def load_wav_librosa(filepath: str, sr: Optional[int] = None, mono: bool = True) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, int]:
    """
    Load WAV file using librosa (with resampling option).
    
    Args:
        filepath: Path to WAV file
        sr: Target sample rate (None = native)
        mono: Convert to mono
    
    Returns:
        Tuple of (audio_data, sample_rate)
    """
    if not HAS_LIBROSA:
        raise AudioLoadError("librosa not available")
    
    audio_data, sample_rate = librosa.load(filepath, sr=sr, mono=mono)
    return audio_data, sample_rate


def load_wav(
    filepath: str,
    *,
    preferred_lib: str = "auto",
    sr: Optional[int] = None,
    mono: bool = False
) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, int]:
    """
    Load WAV file with automatic library selection.
    
    Args:
        filepath: Path to WAV file
        preferred_lib: "auto", "soundfile", "scipy", "librosa", "wave"
        sr: Target sample rate (librosa only)
        mono: Convert to mono (librosa only)
    
    Returns:
        Tuple of (audio_data, sample_rate)
        audio_data: float32 array, shape (n_samples,) for mono or (n_samples, n_channels) for stereo
    """
    filepath = Path(filepath)
    if not filepath.exists():
        raise AudioLoadError(f"File not found: {filepath}")
    
    # Library priority order
    libs = {
        "soundfile": (HAS_SOUNDFILE, load_wav_soundfile),
        "scipy": (HAS_SCIPY, load_wav_scipy),
        "librosa": (HAS_LIBROSA, lambda p: load_wav_librosa(p, sr=sr, mono=mono)),
        "wave": (HAS_WAVE, load_wav_basic),
    }
    
    if preferred_lib != "auto":
        if preferred_lib not in libs:
            raise AudioLoadError(f"Unknown library: {preferred_lib}")
        available, loader = libs[preferred_lib]
        if not available:
            raise AudioLoadError(f"Library not available: {preferred_lib}")
        return loader(str(filepath))
    
    # Auto-select: try in order of preference
    for lib_name, (available, loader) in libs.items():
        if available:
            try:
                return loader(str(filepath))
            except Exception as e:
                warnings.warn(f"{lib_name} failed to load {filepath}: {e}")
                continue
    
    raise AudioLoadError("No audio loading libraries available")


def get_wav_info(filepath: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """
    Get WAV file metadata without loading audio data.
    
    Returns:
        Dict with keys: channels, sample_width, sample_rate, frames, duration, compression
    """
    if not HAS_WAVE:
        raise AudioLoadError("wave module not available")
    
    with wave.open(filepath, 'rb') as wav_file:
        n_channels = wav_file.getnchannels()
        sampwidth = wav_file.getsampwidth()
        framerate = wav_file.getframerate()
        n_frames = wav_file.getnframes()
        comptype = wav_file.getcomptype()
        compname = wav_file.getcompname()
        
        return {
            "channels": n_channels,
            "sample_width_bytes": sampwidth,
            "sample_rate": framerate,
            "num_frames": n_frames,
            "duration_seconds": n_frames / framerate if framerate > 0 else 0,
            "bit_depth": sampwidth * 8,
            "compression_type": comptype,
            "compression_name": compname,
        }


def resample_audio(audio: np.ndarray, orig_sr: int, target_sr: int) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Resample audio to target sample rate.
    
    Requires librosa or scipy.
    """
    if orig_sr == target_sr:
        return audio
    
    if HAS_LIBROSA:
        return librosa.resample(audio, orig_sr=orig_sr, target_sr=target_sr)
    elif HAS_SCIPY:
        from scipy.signal import resample
        n_samples = int(len(audio) * target_sr / orig_sr)
        return resample(audio, n_samples)
    else:
        raise AudioLoadError("Resampling requires librosa or scipy")


def to_mono(audio: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """Convert stereo/multi-channel audio to mono by averaging channels."""
    if audio.ndim == 1:
        return audio
    elif audio.ndim == 2:
        return np.mean(audio, axis=1)
    else:
        raise ValueError(f"Unexpected audio shape: {audio.shape}")


def normalize_audio(audio: np.ndarray, target_db: float = -3.0) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Normalize audio to target dBFS (decibels relative to full scale).
    
    Args:
        audio: Input audio array
        target_db: Target level in dBFS (negative values)
    
    Returns:
        Normalized audio
    """
    rms = np.sqrt(np.mean(audio**2))
    if rms == 0:
        return audio
    
    target_rms = 10**(target_db / 20.0)
    return audio * (target_rms / rms)


# Convenience function for the upload endpoint
def process_uploaded_wav(filepath: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """
    Process an uploaded WAV file: load, validate, and extract basic features.
    
    Returns:
        Dict with audio data, metadata, and basic features
    """
    # Load audio
    audio, sr = load_wav(filepath)
    
    # Get metadata
    info = get_wav_info(filepath)
    
    # Basic features
    duration = len(audio) / sr if audio.ndim == 1 else audio.shape[0] / sr
    rms = float(np.sqrt(np.mean(audio**2)))
    peak = float(np.max(np.abs(audio)))
    
    # Spectral centroid (brightness) - requires librosa
    spectral_centroid = None
    if HAS_LIBROSA:
        try:
            mono_audio = to_mono(audio)
            cent = librosa.feature.spectral_centroid(y=mono_audio, sr=sr)
            spectral_centroid = float(np.mean(cent))
        except Exception:
            pass
    
    return {
        "audio": audio,
        "sample_rate": sr,
        "metadata": info,
        "features": {
            "duration_seconds": duration,
            "rms_level": rms,
            "peak_level": peak,
            "spectral_centroid_hz": spectral_centroid,
            "num_channels": audio.shape[1] if audio.ndim == 2 else 1,

backend/src/app/audio/processor.py
```python
import os
import numpy as np
from typing import Tuple, Optional, Dict, Any
import logging

# Try to import audio processing libraries
try:
    import librosa
    LIBROSA_AVAILABLE = True
except ImportError:
    LIBROSA_AVAILABLE = False

try:
    from scipy.io import wavfile
    SCIPY_AVAILABLE = True
except ImportError:
    SCIPY_AVAILABLE = False

try:
    import soundfile as sf
    SOUNDFILE_AVAILABLE = True
except ImportError:
    SOUNDFILE_AVAILABLE = False

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

class AudioProcessor:
    """Handles loading and processing of WAV audio files."""
    
    def __init__(self, target_sr: int = 22050):
        """
        Initialize audio processor.
        
        Args:
            target_sr: Target sample rate for processing (default 22050 Hz)
        """
        self.target_sr = target_sr
        self._check_dependencies()
    
    def _check_dependencies(self):
        """Check which audio libraries are available."""
        available = []
        if LIBROSA_AVAILABLE:
            available.append("librosa")
        if SCIPY_AVAILABLE:
            available.append("scipy")
        if SOUNDFILE_AVAILABLE:
            available.append("soundfile")
        
        if not available:
            logger.warning("No audio processing libraries found. Install librosa, scipy, or soundfile.")
        else:
            logger.info(f"Audio processing libraries available: {', '.join(available)}")
    
    def load_wav(self, filepath: str) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, int]:
        """
        Load WAV file and return audio data and sample rate.
        
        Args:
            filepath: Path to WAV file
            
        Returns:
            Tuple of (audio_data, sample_rate)
            audio_data is mono float32 array normalized to [-1, 1]
        """
        if not os.path.exists(filepath):
            raise FileNotFoundError(f"WAV file not found: {filepath}")
        
        # Try different libraries in order of preference
        if LIBROSA_AVAILABLE:
            return self._load_with_librosa(filepath)
        elif SOUNDFILE_AVAILABLE:
            return self._load_with_soundfile(filepath)
        elif SCIPY_AVAILABLE:
            return self._load_with_scipy(filepath)
        else:
            raise RuntimeError("No audio processing library available. Install librosa, soundfile, or scipy.")
    
    def _load_with_librosa(self, filepath: str) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, int]:
        """Load using librosa (resamples to target_sr)."""
        audio, sr = librosa.load(filepath, sr=self.target_sr, mono=True)
        return audio.astype(np.float32), sr
    
    def _load_with_soundfile(self, filepath: str) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, int]:
        """Load using soundfile."""
        audio, sr = sf.read(filepath, dtype='float32')
        # Convert to mono if stereo
        if len(audio.shape) > 1:
            audio = np.mean(audio, axis=1)
        # Resample if needed
        if sr != self.target_sr and LIBROSA_AVAILABLE:
            audio = librosa.resample(audio, orig_sr=sr, target_sr=self.target_sr)
            sr = self.target_sr
        return audio, sr
    
    def _load_with_scipy(self, filepath: str) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, int]:
        """Load using scipy."""
        sr, audio = wavfile.read(filepath)
        # Convert to float32 and normalize
        if audio.dtype == np.int16:
            audio = audio.astype(np.float32) / 32768.0
        elif audio.dtype == np.int32:
            audio = audio.astype(np.float32) / 2147483648.0
        elif audio.dtype == np.uint8:
            audio = (audio.astype(np.float32) - 128) / 128.0
        else:
            audio = audio.astype(np.float32)
        
        # Convert to mono if stereo
        if len(audio.shape) > 1:
            audio = np.mean(audio, axis=1)
        
        # Resample if needed (requires librosa)
        if sr != self.target_sr:
            if LIBROSA_AVAILABLE:
                audio = librosa.resample(audio, orig_sr=sr, target_sr=self.target_sr)
                sr = self.target_sr
            else:
                logger.warning(f"Sample rate mismatch: file is {sr}Hz, target is {self.target_sr}Hz. "
                             "Install librosa for automatic resampling.")
        
        return audio, sr
    
    def get_audio_info(self, filepath: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Get basic info about WAV file without loading full audio.
        
        Returns:
            Dict with keys: duration, sample_rate, channels, frames, format
        """
        if SOUNDFILE_AVAILABLE:
            info = sf.info(filepath)
            return {
                "duration": info.duration,
                "sample_rate": info.samplerate,
                "channels": info.channels,
                "frames": info.frames,
                "format": info.format,
                "subtype": info.subtype
            }
        elif SCIPY_AVAILABLE:
            sr, audio = wavfile.read(filepath)
            duration = len(audio) / sr
            channels = 1 if len(audio.shape) == 1 else audio.shape[1]
            return {
                "duration": duration,
                "sample_rate": sr,
                "channels": channels,
                "frames": len(audio),
                "format": "WAV",
                "subtype": str(audio.dtype)
            }
        else:
            # Fallback: load with librosa to get info
            if LIBROSA_AVAILABLE:
                audio, sr = librosa.load(filepath, sr=None, mono=False)
                duration = len(audio) / sr if len(audio.shape) == 1 else audio.shape[1] / sr
                channels = 1 if len(audio.shape) == 1 else audio.shape[0]
                return {
                    "duration": duration,
                    "sample_rate": sr,
                    "channels": channels,
                    "frames": len(audio) if len(audio.shape) == 1 else audio.shape[1],
                    "format": "WAV",
                    "subtype": "unknown"
                }
            raise RuntimeError("No audio library available to get file info")
    
    def process_audio(self, filepath: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Process WAV file and return analysis results.
        
        This is a placeholder for actual audio processing logic.
        Extend this method based on your specific needs (pitch detection, 
        onset detection, feature extraction, etc.)
        
        Returns:
            Dict with processing results
        """
        audio, sr = self.load_wav(filepath)
        info = self.get_audio_info(filepath)
        
        # Basic analysis
        duration = len(audio) / sr
        rms_energy = np.sqrt(np.mean(audio**2))
        max_amplitude = np.max(np.abs(audio))
        
        # Placeholder for more advanced processing
        results = {
            "filepath": filepath,
            "duration": duration,
            "sample_rate": sr,
            "num_samples": len(audio),
            "rms_energy": float(rms_energy),
            "max_amplitude": float(max_amplitude),
            "is_silent": rms_energy < 0.001,  # Threshold for silence detection
            "info": info
        }
        
        logger.info(f"Processed audio: {filepath} ({duration:.2f}s, {sr}Hz, RMS: {rms_energy:.4f})")
        return results


# Convenience function for simple usage
def load_and_process_wav(filepath: str, target_sr: int = 22050) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """
    Load and process a WAV file in one call.
    
    Args:
        filepath: Path to WAV file
        target_sr: Target sample rate
        
    Returns:
        Dict with processing results
    """
    processor = AudioProcessor(target_sr=target_sr)
    return processor.process_audio(filepath)
```

Now let me update the upload route to use this new processor:

backend/src/app/routes/main.py
```python
import os
from flask import Blueprint, jsonify, current_app, request
from werkzeug.utils import secure_filename

# Import the new audio processor
from ..audio.processor import AudioProcessor, load_and_process_wav

bp = Blueprint('main', __name__)

def allowed_file(filename):
    """Check if file has allowed extension."""
    return '.' in filename and \
           filename.rsplit('.', 1)[1].lower() in current_app.config['UPLOAD_EXTENSIONS']

@bp.route('/')
def index():
    return jsonify({
        "status": "ok", 
        "message": "ECKO Backend Running",
        "environment": current_app.config.get('ENV', 'unknown')
    })

@bp.route('/health')
def health():
    # Add DB check here if needed: db.session.execute(text('SELECT 1'))
    return jsonify({"status": "healthy"}), 200

@bp.route('/upload', methods=['POST'])
def upload_wav():
    """Endpoint to receive .wav file from frontend."""
    # Check if file part exists in request
    if 'file' not in request.files:
        return jsonify({"error": "No file part in request"}), 400
    
    file = request.files['file']
    
    # Check if file was selected
    if file.filename == '':
        return jsonify({"error": "No file selected"}), 400
    
    # Validate file extension
    if not allowed_file(file.filename):
        return jsonify({
            "error": "Invalid file type. Only .wav files are allowed.",
            "allowed_extensions": current_app.config['UPLOAD_EXTENSIONS']
        }), 400
    
    # Secure filename and save
    filename = secure_filename(file.filename)
    upload_folder = current_app.config['UPLOAD_FOLDER']
    filepath = os.path.join(upload_folder, filename)
    
    try:
        file.save(filepath)
        file_size = os.path.getsize(filepath)
        
        # Process the audio file
        try:
            # Initialize processor with config sample rate (default 22050)
            target_sr = current_app.config.get('AUDIO_TARGET_SR', 22050)
            processor = AudioProcessor(target_sr=target_sr)
            processing_results = processor.process_audio(filepath)
            
            # Add processing results to response
            response_data = {
                "status": "success",
                "message": "File uploaded and processed successfully",
                "filename": filename,
                "size_bytes": file_size,
                "size_mb": round(file_size / (1024 * 1024), 2),
                "saved_path": filepath,
                "audio_analysis": processing_results
            }
        except Exception as proc_error:
            current_app.logger.error(f"Audio processing failed: {str(proc_error)}")
            # Still return success for upload, but note processing failure
            response_data = {
                "status": "success",
                "message": "File uploaded but processing failed",
                "filename": filename,
                "size_bytes": file_size,
                "size_mb": round(file_size / (1024 * 1024), 2),
                "saved_path": filepath,
                "processing_error": str(proc_error)
            }
        
        return jsonify(response_data), 201
        
    except Exception as e:
        current_app.logger.error(f"File upload failed: {str(e)}")
        return jsonify({"error": "File upload failed", "details": str(e)}), 500
```

The new `AudioProcessor` class provides:
1. **Flexible loading** - Uses librosa, soundfile, or scipy (in order of preference)
2. **Automatic resampling** - Converts to target sample rate (default 22050 Hz)
3. **Mono conversion** - Handles stereo files by averaging channels
4. **Basic analysis** - Duration, RMS energy, silence detection
5. **Extensible design** - Easy to add pitch detection, onset detection, MFCCs, etc.

The upload endpoint now:
1. Saves the file as before
2. Processes it with `AudioProcessor`
3. Returns audio analysis in the response
4. Gracefully handles processing failures without failing the upload

        # Resample if needed (requires librosa)
        if sr != self.target_sr:
            if LIBROSA_AVAILABLE:
                audio = librosa.resample(audio, orig_sr=sr, target_sr=self.target_sr)
                sr = self.target_sr
            else:
                logger.warning(f"Sample rate mismatch: file is {sr}Hz, target is {self.target_sr}Hz. "
                             "Install librosa for automatic resampling.")
        
        return audio, sr
    
    def get_audio_info(self, filepath: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Get basic info about WAV file without loading full audio.
        
        Returns:
            Dict with keys: duration, sample_rate, channels, frames, format
        """
        if SOUNDFILE_AVAILABLE:
            info = sf.info(filepath)
            return {
                "duration": info.duration,
                "sample_rate": info.samplerate,
                "channels": info.channels,
                "frames": info.frames,
                "format": info.format,
                "subtype": info.subtype
            }
        elif SCIPY_AVAILABLE:
            sr, audio = wavfile.read(filepath)
            duration = len(audio) / sr
            channels = 1 if len(audio.shape) == 1 else audio.shape[1]
            return {
                "duration": duration,
                "sample_rate": sr,
                "channels": channels,
                "frames": len(audio),
                "format": "WAV",
                "subtype": str(audio.dtype)
            }
        else:
            # Fallback: load with librosa to get info
            if LIBROSA_AVAILABLE:
                audio, sr = librosa.load(filepath, sr=None, mono=False)
                duration = len(audio) / sr if len(audio.shape) == 1 else audio.shape[1] / sr
                channels = 1 if len(audio.shape) == 1 else audio.shape[0]
                return {
                    "duration": duration,
                    "sample_rate": sr,
                    "channels": channels,
                    "frames": len(audio) if len(audio.shape) == 1 else audio.shape[1],
                    "format": "WAV",
                    "subtype": "unknown"
                }
            raise RuntimeError("No audio library available to get file info")
    
    def detect_sound_segments(self, audio: np.ndarray, sr: int, 
                              top_db: float = 30, 
                              min_duration: float = 0.1) -> List[Dict[str, float]]:
        """
        Detect segments where sound is present (non-silent regions).
        
        Args:
            audio: Audio signal
            sr: Sample rate
            top_db: Threshold in decibels below reference to consider as silence
            min_duration: Minimum duration of a sound segment in seconds
            
        Returns:
            List of dicts with 'start', 'end', 'duration' for each sound segment
        """
        if not LIBROSA_AVAILABLE:
            logger.warning("librosa not available, cannot detect sound segments")
            return [{"start": 0.0, "end": len(audio) / sr, "duration": len(audio) / sr}]
        
        # Use librosa's silence detection
        intervals = librosa.effects.split(
            audio, 
            top_db=top_db,
            frame_length=self.frame_length,
            hop_length=self.hop_length
        )
        
        # Convert frame indices to time
        segments = []
        for start_frame, end_frame in intervals:
            start_time = start_frame / sr
            end_time = end_frame / sr
            duration = end_time - start_time
            
            if duration >= min_duration:
                segments.append({
                    "start": float(start_time),
                    "end": float(end_time),
                    "duration": float(duration)
                })
        
        return segments
    
    def extract_pitch(self, audio: np.ndarray, sr: int,
                      fmin: float = 80.0, fmax: float = 800.0) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Extract fundamental frequency (pitch) using PYIN algorithm.
        
        Args:
            audio: Audio signal
            sr: Sample rate
            fmin: Minimum frequency to detect
            fmax: Maximum frequency to detect
            
        Returns:
            Dict with pitch information
        """
        if not LIBROSA_AVAILABLE:
            logger.warning("librosa not available, cannot extract pitch")
            return {"frequencies": [], "times": [], "mean_hz": 0.0, "median_hz": 0.0}
        
        # Use PYIN for pitch detection
        f0, voiced_flag, voiced_probs = librosa.pyin(
            audio,
            fmin=fmin,
            fmax=fmax,
            sr=sr,
            frame_length=self.frame_length,
            hop_length=self.hop_length
        )
        
        # Get time stamps for each frame
        times = librosa.frames_to_time(
            np.arange(len(f0)), 
            sr=sr, 
            hop_length=self.hop_length
        )
        
        # Filter only voiced frames
        voiced_f0 = f0[voiced_flag]
        voiced_times = times[voiced_flag]
        
        result = {
            "frequencies": f0.tolist(),
            "times": times.tolist(),
            "voiced_flag": voiced_flag.tolist(),
            "voiced_probabilities": voiced_probs.tolist(),
            "mean_hz": float(np.nanmean(voiced_f0)) if len(voiced_f0) > 0 else 0.0,
            "median_hz": float(np.nanmedian(voiced_f0)) if len(voiced_f0) > 0 else 0.0,
            "min_hz": float(np.nanmin(voiced_f0)) if len(voiced_f0) > 0 else 0.0,
            "max_hz": float(np.nanmax(voiced_f0)) if len(voiced_f0) > 0 else 0.0,
            "voiced_duration": float(np.sum(voiced_flag) * self.hop_length / sr),
            "total_frames": len(f0),
            "voiced_frames": int(np.sum(voiced_flag))
        }
        
        return result
    
    def extract_volume_envelope(self, audio: np.ndarray, sr: int) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Extract volume (amplitude) envelope using RMS energy.
        
        Args:
            audio: Audio signal
            sr: Sample rate
            
        Returns:
            Dict with volume envelope information
        """
        if not LIBROSA_AVAILABLE:
            logger.warning("librosa not available, cannot extract volume envelope")
            # Fallback: simple RMS per frame
            frame_length = min(self.frame_length, len(audio))
            hop_length = min(self.hop_length, frame_length // 4)
            rms_values = []
            times = []
            for i in range(0, len(audio) - frame_length, hop_length):
                frame = audio[i:i + frame_length]
                rms = np.sqrt(np.mean(frame**2))
                rms_values.append(float(rms))
                times.append(float(i / sr))
            return {
                "rms_values": rms_values,
                "times": times,
                "mean_rms": float(np.mean(rms_values)) if rms_values else 0.0,
                "max_rms": float(np.max(rms_values)) if rms_values else 0.0
            }
        
        # Compute RMS energy
        rms = librosa.feature.rms(
            y=audio,
            frame_length=self.frame_length,
            hop_length=self.hop_length
        )[0]
        
        # Convert to dB
        rms_db = librosa.amplitude_to_db(rms, ref=np.max)
        
        # Get time stamps
        times = librosa.frames_to_time(
            np.arange(len(rms)), 
            sr=sr, 
            hop_length=self.hop_length
        )
        
        return {
            "rms_values": rms.tolist(),
            "rms_db": rms_db.tolist(),
            "times": times.tolist(),
            "mean_rms": float(np.mean(rms)),
            "max_rms": float(np.max(rms)),
            "mean_db": float(np.mean(rms_db)),
            "max_db": float(np.max(rms_db)),
            "dynamic_range_db": float(np.max(rms_db) - np.min(rms_db))
        }
    
    def extract_spectral_features(self, audio: np.ndarray, sr: int) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Extract additional spectral features.
        
        Args:
            audio: Audio signal
            sr: Sample rate
            
        Returns:
            Dict with spectral features
        """
        if not LIBROSA_AVAILABLE:
            return {}
        
        # Spectral centroid (brightness)
        centroid = librosa.feature.spectral_centroid(
            y=audio, sr=sr, hop_length=self.hop_length
        )[0]
        
        # Spectral rolloff
        rolloff = librosa.feature.spectral_rolloff(
            y=audio, sr=sr, hop_length=self.hop_length
        )[0]
        
        # Spectral bandwidth
        bandwidth = librosa.feature.spectral_bandwidth(
            y=audio, sr=sr, hop_length=self.hop_length
        )[0]
        
        # Zero crossing rate
        zcr = librosa.feature.zero_crossing_rate(
            audio, frame_length=self.frame_length, hop_length=self.hop_length
        )[0]
        
        times = librosa.frames_to_time(
            np.arange(len(centroid)), 
            sr=sr, 
            hop_length=self.hop_length
        )
        
        return {
            "spectral_centroid": {
                "values": centroid.tolist(),
                "times": times.tolist(),
                "mean": float(np.mean(centroid)),
                "std": float(np.std(centroid))
            },
            "spectral_rolloff": {
                "values": rolloff.tolist(),
                "mean": float(np.mean(rolloff)),
                "std": float(np.std(rolloff))
            },
            "spectral_bandwidth": {
                "values": bandwidth.tolist(),
                "mean": float(np.mean(bandwidth)),
                "std": float(np.std(bandwidth))
            },
            "zero_crossing_rate": {
                "values": zcr.tolist(),
                "mean": float(np.mean(zcr)),
                "std": float(np.std(zcr))
            }
        }
    
    def process_audio(self, filepath: str, 
                      detect_segments: bool = True,
                      extract_pitch: bool = True,
                      extract_volume: bool = True,
                      extract_spectral: bool = True) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        """
        Process WAV file and return comprehensive analysis results.
        
        Args:
            filepath: Path to WAV file
            detect_segments: Whether to detect sound/silence segments
            extract_pitch: Whether to extract pitch (fundamental frequency)
            extract_volume: Whether to extract volume envelope
            extract_spectral: Whether to extract spectral features
            
        Returns:
            Dict with processing results
        """
        audio, sr = self.load_wav(filepath)
        info = self.get_audio_info(filepath)
        
        # Basic analysis
        duration = len(audio) / sr
        rms_energy = np.sqrt(np.mean(audio**2))
        max_amplitude = np.max(np.abs(audio))
        
        results = {
            "filepath": filepath,
            "duration": float(duration),
            "sample_rate": sr,
            "num_samples": len(audio),
            "rms_energy": float(rms_energy),
            "max_amplitude": float(max_amplitude),
            "is_silent": rms_energy < 0.001,
            "info": info
        }
        
        # Detect sound segments (onset/offset)
        if detect_segments:
            segments = self.detect_sound_segments(audio, sr)
            total_sound_duration = sum(s["duration"] for s in segments)
            results["sound_segments"] = segments
            results["total_sound_duration"] = float(total_sound_duration)
            results["silence_ratio"] = float(1.0 - total_sound_duration / duration) if duration > 0 else 1.0
            results["num_segments"] = len(segments)
        
        # Extract pitch
        if extract_pitch:
            pitch_data = self.extract_pitch(audio, sr)
            results["pitch"] = pitch_data
        
        # Extract volume envelope
        if extract_volume:
            volume_data = self.extract_volume_envelope(audio, sr)
            results["volume"] = volume_data
        
        # Extract spectral features
        if extract_spectral:
            spectral_data = self.extract_spectral_features(audio, sr)
            results["spectral"] = spectral_data
        
        logger.info(f"Processed audio: {filepath} ({duration:.2f}s, {sr}Hz, RMS: {rms_energy:.4f})")
        return results


# Convenience function for simple usage
def load_and_process_wav(filepath: str, target_sr: int = 22050, **kwargs) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """
    Load and process a WAV file in one call.
    
    Args:
        filepath: Path to WAV file
        target_sr: Target sample rate
        **kwargs: Additional arguments passed to process_audio()
        
    Returns:
        Dict with processing results
    """
    processor = AudioProcessor(target_sr=target_sr)
    return processor.process_audio(filepath, **kwargs)
